## Document 272: Continue To Proceed With Caution

Source PDF: all report pdf/Continue To Proceed With Caution.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Continue To Proceed With Caution
Date:    8 hours ago
Continue To Proceed With Caution

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Continue To Proceed With
Caution
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 14 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Muhammad Daudy / Unsplash

I. Risk Off Is Back
On September 15, we advised our readers to "Proceed With Caution." We
postponed our year-end S&P 500 target of 8,400 to mid-2027. Our
concern isn't earnings; it's stock valuations.

### Page 2

We remain bullish on the economy and earnings, expecting no recession
through the end of the decade in 2029. Accordingly, we haven’t lowered
our 2027 S&P 500 earnings forecast of $425 per share, which is only
slightly above the industry analysts' current consensus of $423.08 (chart).
Instead, we lowered our year-end forward P/E forecast from 19.8 to 18.6;
that’s what reduced our year-end S&P 500 target to 7,900. So far, our
caution has been warranted. The S&P 500's forward P/E has been moving

toward our targeted valuation multiple (chart).
Risk-off sentiment was on full display in the stock market today for most of
the reasons we flagged in mid-September. The S&P 500 fell 1.1% to
7,718.94, while the Nasdaq Composite and Russell 2000 both dropped
1.9%. Selling pressure was particularly pronounced among technology

### Page 3

stocks, with the SOXX tumbling 4.5% (chart). Interestingly, IGV fell 3.0%,
while MAGS was down only 1.9%.
The AI trade is getting sloppy amid intensifying competition and doubts
about returns on massive AI investments. Shares of heavily indebted
neocloud provider CoreWeave plunged 16% today amid growing concerns
about its ability to service its debt (chart). Furthermore, today's CPI was
hotter than expected, raising the odds of two Fed rate hikes over the rest

of this year. A cargo ship sank in the Strait of Hormuz after hitting an
Iranian mine.
Meanwhile, the risk-off sentiment we anticipated may be helping
Treasuries find their footing. On Saturday, we suggested that the 10-year
Treasury yield might stabilize around 5.25%. So far, that call is holding up

### Page 4

despite mounting fears of further Fed tightening today (chart). That's partly
because Treasuries offer attractive real returns, with the 10-year TIPS
yielding around 2.90% (chart).
Our worry list remains long, including more partisanship after the midterm
elections and a re-escalation of the war in the Middle East. We expect
risk-off sentiment to persist, further compressing valuation multiples.
That's why we still recommend proceeding with caution. On the other

hand, buying opportunities are increasing, and Santa could still deliver a
year-end rally up to 7,900.
II. Inflation Is Back Too
Today's September CPI report fueled bets on further Fed tightening. The
probability of an October rate hike jumped from 21% to 36%, briefly
reaching 45%. Meanwhile, the 2-year Treasury yield surged as much as
13bps intraday before closing 3bps higher at 4.79%, roughly 95 bps above
the midpoint of the federal funds target range (chart).

### Page 5

Here's more:
(1) CPI inflation (m/m). The headline CPI jumped 0.6% m/m in
September, its biggest increase since March, led by a 9.3% surge in
energy prices (chart). But inflationary pressures were broad-based, with
the core CPI rising 0.3%, including gains of 0.3% in core goods and 0.4%
in core services. Indeed, 52% of CPI categories recorded inflation above
3% y/y, with 70% above 2%.
Durable goods prices jumped 0.9% m/m in September (chart). Notably,

prices of computers, peripherals, and smart home assistants rose 1.3%,
pushing this three-month moving average inflation rate to a record high.
That reflects surging semiconductor prices driven by the AI investment

### Page 6

boom. Tariffs are also adding to inflation, with prices of other household
equipment and furnishings jumping 2.1% m/m.
Nondurable goods prices jumped 1.4% m/m in September, led by a 9.3%
surge in motor fuel prices (chart).
The services sector also showed broad-based inflationary pressures, with
prices rising 0.4% m/m (chart). Higher energy costs are passing through
to transportation and travel services, while robust aggregate demand is
sustaining pricing power.

### Page 7

(2) CPI inflation (y/y). Both headline and core inflation accelerated on a
y/y basis in September, marking the 67th consecutive month that inflation
has remained above the Fed's 2% target (chart).
Both goods and services inflation rose in September (chart). With durable
goods deflation unlikely to return soon, services inflation must fall more to
bring inflation back to target. However, services inflation remains
stubbornly elevated.

### Page 8

Supercore CPI inflation rose to 3.2% y/y in September, underscoring the
persistence of underlying price pressures (chart).
III. The Fed's Beige Book
The Fed's October Beige Book, which summarizes economic conditions
across the 12 Federal Reserve districts, reinforced the September CPI
report's message that inflation remains troublesome.
Half the districts reported robust or accelerating price pressures,
compared with broadly stable inflation in September. Four districts

reported accelerating inflation, while three characterized price growth as
robust, up from one previously. Higher energy costs, tariffs, and insurance
premiums were the main drivers, with diesel costs passing through via

### Page 9

freight surcharges (chart). Expiring hedges and contract renewals threaten
further increases.
Meanwhile, economic activity remains robust, supported by strong
manufacturing demand from AI data centers, defense, and energy.

of the QuickTakes article. Paid members' contributions may be
featured in our segment, "Ed Answers Your Questions".

## Document 274: CoreWeave信贷危机更像2023年SVB恐慌，而非2008年次贷危机？

Source PDF: all report pdf/CoreWeave信贷危机更像2023年SVB恐慌，而非2008年次贷危机？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

当前的关键不是 CoreWeave 会不会倒，⽽是算⼒定价能否⽀撑起这个周期的融资逻辑。这
个问题的答案，将决定这场恐慌的最终归宿。
摘要：
CoreWeave ⽆担保债券收益率飙升⾄约 15% 、 CDS 利差突破 1000 个基点，令市场对整个 AI 资本
⽀出周期的系统性⻛险产⽣强烈质疑。
然⽽，华尔街头部机构正集体发声：这不是 2007 年新世纪⾦融（ New Century Financial ）式的
系统性崩塌，⽽更像 2023 年硅⾕银⾏（ SVB ）引发的阶段性恐慌 —— ⼀只规模极⼩、结构极脆
弱的⼯具，正被市场当作万亿美元 AI 资本⽀出周期的晴⾬表。
巴克莱全球宏观研究主管 Ajay Rajadhyaksha 指出， CoreWeave 约 15 亿美元的⽆担保债券在总
债务约 350 亿美元中属于结构性次级地位，以此⼯具的价格信号否定整个 AI 周期，与 2023 年⽤⼀
只约 40 亿美元的区域银⾏ ETF 预判美国经济衰退如出⼀辙。⾼盛则认为市场选错了参照系 ——

⽆担保债券市场并不代表 CoreWeave 真实的新增融资成本。
历史先例： SVB 还是 07 年⾦融危机？
这场争论的核⼼，是历史类⽐的选择。
2007 年 4 ⽉，美国最⼤次级抵押贷款机构新世纪⾦融申请破产，其后整个信贷市场逐步崩塌，标
普 500 指数在随后两年下跌约 40% 。 2023 年 3 ⽉，硅⾕银⾏被监管当局关闭，市场随即形成叙
事：区域银⾏是中⼩企业信贷命脉，信贷枯竭将触发衰退。债券市场迅速定价当年降息 75 ⾄ 100
个基点， VIX 恐慌指数飙升程度甚⾄超过 2007 年。
结果，美联储 2023 年⼀次未降息，反⽽继续加息，美国经济全年增速远超预期，衰退从未到
来。区域银⾏ ETF KRE 收复全部失地，标普 500 在随后两年上涨约 40% 。
彭博专栏作家 John Authers 指出，两次危机在实时均貌似严峻，最终⾛向却南辕北辙。SVB 是
⼀次被过度外推的恐慌，⽽⾮系统性⻛险的前兆；新世纪⾦融的破产则揭⽰了⼀个贯穿整个⾦
融体系的结构性缺陷。当前 CoreWeave 的处境，从形态上更接近 SVB 。

Rajadhyaksha 总结了 SVB 恐慌的核⼼错误： " 错误不在于认识到硅⾕银⾏已经倒闭。它确实倒
了。错误在于从⼀个⼩型、波动剧烈、结构脆弱的⼯具，外推⾄整个美国经济。 "
巴克莱： 15 亿美元的 " 尾巴 " 在摇动整条 " 狗 "
Rajadhyaksha 将当前市场恐慌的逻辑结构与 SVB 时期直接类⽐。
2023 年那条 " 尾巴 " ，是⼀只资产管理规模约 40 亿美元的等权重区域银⾏ ETF——KRE ，许多成
分股交投清淡。就是这只⼩ ETF 的下跌，带动了数万亿美元的利率、投资级信⽤和股票仓位重新
定价，形成⾃我强化的恐慌循环。
2026 年的 " 尾巴 " ，则是 CoreWeave 约 15 亿美元的结构性次级⽆担保债券。
Rajadhyaksha 梳理了 CoreWeave 的三层债务结构：最主要的是有担保的延迟提款定期贷款
（ DDTL ），存放于独⽴特殊⽬的载体（ SPV ）中，每个 SPV 背后绑定具体的 GPU 硬件、客⼾合
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

同和数据中⼼租约 —— 当 Meta 签署多年期 " ⽤不⽤都要付钱 " 的合同并转让给 SPV ，贷款的实质
抵押品是 Meta 的付款承诺；其次是约 90 亿美元的可转换债券，票息介于 1.75% ⾄ 2.875% ；最底
层才是⽆担保票据，清算时前⾯有数百亿美元债权排队。
" 1 5 % 的 收 益 率 告 诉 你 这 张 纸 的 ⼀ 些 信 息 ， 它 不 能 告 诉 你 这 家 公 司 即 将 停 ⽌ 运
营， "Rajadhyaksha 写道，并打了⼀个⽐⽅： " 如果⼀个⼈有 3500 万美元的房贷加上⼀张⼩额信
⽤卡，信⽤卡利率⾼，说明的是信⽤卡的问题，不能说明这座城市所有房贷借款⼈都有⿇烦。 "
数字对⽐同样直观： 2023 年 3 ⽉，市场因⼀只 40 亿美元的 ETF 下跌定价 100 个基点降息； 2026
年，市场因约 15 亿美元的结构性次级债券交投于 80 美分，开始质疑⼀个万亿美元级别的资本⽀
出周期。
⾼盛：市场选错了融资成本的参照系
⾼盛分析师 Gabriela Borges 和 Maura Hager 的核⼼论点，同样落在债务结构的误读上。

⾼盛指出，CoreWeave 的主要融资⼯具是 DDTL—— 以特定客⼾合同、 GPU 基础设施及相关现
⾦流为抵押的资产⽀持项⽬融资。贷款⽅承保的是具体合同的现⾦流质量，⽽⾮ CoreWeave 的
整体信⽤状况，这⼀结构将融资⻛险从公司资产负债表转移⾄底层资产，并降低了再融资⻛
险。⽆担保债券市场对 CoreWeave 的新发⾏已关闭数⽉，以该市场的收益率衡量融资成本，等
同于参照⼀个已失效的价格信号。
基于⼆季度利息⽀出年化 25.6 亿美元除以约 300 亿美元总债务，⾼盛估算 CoreWeave 当前加权平
均债务成本约为 800 ⾄ 900 个基点。对于新增融资，⾼盛假设投资级客⼾（如 Meta ）对应 DDTL
定价约为 SOFR+300 个基点，⾼收益客⼾约为 SOFR+600 个基点，混合成本区间 750 ⾄ 1050 个
基点，保守基准取 1000 个基点，意味着新增融资成本较当前仅上升 100 ⾄ 200 个基点。
⾼盛还援引市场数据： DDTL 5.0 和 5.5 均以不低于原始发⾏折价的价格交易，表明资产⽀持融资

市场对 CoreWeave 的信⼼尚未崩塌。
值得注意的是，⾼盛在报告披露附录中说明，其正担任 CoreWeave ⼀项待定承销业务的主承销
商或联席承销商，持有 CoreWeave 逾 1% 股份，过去 12 个⽉已从 CoreWeave 获得投资银⾏业务
报酬，并预计在未来三个⽉继续寻求相关业务。
⾼盛⽜市逻辑的⽀点与脆弱性：每兆⽡收⼊需提升 30%
⾼盛⽜市论点的另⼀半，建⽴在 CoreWeave 计算收费能⼒的上升预期之上。
⾼盛估算， CoreWeave ⽬前混合货币化⽔平约为每兆⽡年度经常性收⼊（ ARR ） 1000 万美元。
⽀撑上⾏的依据包括：短期合同（ 3 ⾄ 6 个⽉）签约价已达每兆⽡ 4000 万美元； CoreWeave 今年
7 ⽉提价 25% ， 10 ⽉再度提价 10% ；⼆季度签署的合同贡献利润率⽐近期合同⾼出 5 ⾄ 10 个百分
点。⾼盛将每兆⽡ 1300 万美元作为新签⻓期合同的 " 合理起点假设 " 。
代⼊⽰例模型（ 5 年期、 200 兆⽡合同， 80% 债务融资，融资成本 10% ，残值为零），可得 15% 的

⽆杠杆内部收益率，与 11.8% 的加权平均资本成本之间形成约 3 个百分点的正利差。
然⽽⾼盛的敏感性分析同样揭⽰了这⼀逻辑的脆弱性：若以当前实际混合⽔平 1000 万美元 / 兆⽡
计算，⽆杠杆内部收益率将跌⾄ 6.3% ，在同等融资成本下利差变为负 6 个百分点。整个⽜市逻辑
的成⽴，依赖于 CoreWeave 每兆⽡收⼊在现有基础上提升约 30% 。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
⾼盛⾃⾝模型数据也揭⽰了这条路径的漫⻓： 2026 年和 2027 年⾃由现⾦流均为负 282 亿美元，
2028 年为负 158 亿美元，⻓期债务将从今年的约 440 亿美元攀升⾄ 2028 年的约 910 亿美元，预计
2030 年⾃由现⾦流才能转正。
供应链数据：需求现实是 AI 周期的真正 " 兜底 "
Rajadhyaksha 将视线拉向供应链最深处，以硬数据反驳系统性崩塌的叙事。
台积电近期报告营收超出⾃⾝指引上限 10 亿美元，连续第六个季度创利润纪录，⾼性能计算营
收占⽐升⾄ 68% ，公司上调四季度增⻓指引。 ASML 同期录得创纪录销售额，营业利润翻倍以
上， CEO Christophe Fouquet 表⽰ 2027 年和 2028 年的计划供应量将⽆法完全满⾜客⼾需求，部
分客⼾已开始尝试预订 2029 年和 2030 年的设备。
五⼤超⼤规模云⼚商（微软、 Meta 、 Alphabet 、亚⻢逊、甲⻣⽂） 2026 年 AI 基础设施合计⽀出

约 7250 亿⾄ 8000 亿美元， 2027 年估算超过 1 万亿美元。 CoreWeave 的 2026 年资本⽀出指引约为
350 亿⾄ 390 亿美元，约占上述总量的 4% ⾄ 5% 。
" 即 便 C o r e W e a v e 完 全 停 ⽌⽀ 出 ， 我 们 损 失 的 也 只 是整 个 A I 资 本⽀ 出 的 4 % ⾄
5% ， "Rajadhyaksha 表⽰， " 超⼤规模云⼚商的⽀出主要依靠⾃由现⾦流，⽽⾮⽆担保债券市
场。从⼀家新兴云⼚商外推⾄全球最赚钱的公司之⼀，这个逻辑链条拉得太⻓了。 "
巴克莱也承认，这次与 2023 年存在⼀个关键差异：没有等价的央⾏⼯具可以充当安全⽹。2023
年，美联储推出银⾏定期融资计划（ BTFP ），允许银⾏以⾯值质押⽔下债券换取流动性，成为
打断恐慌循环的关键。 AI 基础设施融资没有类似的政策⼯具。但 Rajadhyaksha 认为，超⼤规模
云⼚商对算⼒的真实需求本⾝，构成了这⼀周期⾃⼰的 " 兜底逻辑 " 。
Rajadhyaksha 坦⾔： " 市场有⼀种倾向，总是在复杂系统中找到最⼩、最波动的⼯具，然后把它

当作整体的晴⾬表。 2023 年是 KRE ， 2026 年是 CoreWeave 的⽆担保债券。两次信号都是真实
的。 2023 年的外推是错的，我们认为 2026 年的外推同样是错的。 "
真正的⻛险触发点：算⼒租⾦能否持守
尽管华尔街头部机构集体 " 灭⽕ " ，但各⽅均明确了⼀个共同的⻛险触发条件。
Rajadhyaksha 指出，AI 资本⽀出周期是否重蹈次贷覆辙，关键在于算⼒租⾦能否维持在⾜以覆
盖资本成本的⽔平。 " 若租⾦未能如预期那般⾼，可能引发真正的危机。 CoreWeave 是⼀个令⼈
警觉的情况，但它并不能提供这⽅⾯的证据。 "
⾼盛的敏感性分析同样指向这⼀核⼼脆弱性：若每兆⽡ ARR ⽆法从当前 1000 万美元升⾄ 1300 万
美元，整个正利差逻辑将全⾯逆转。⽽⼀旦供给追上需求、定价回落， CoreWeave 仍被锁定在
⻓达 7 ⾄ 16 年的数据中⼼租约中，退出代价⾼昂。
Equity Armor Investments 的 Brian Stutland 认为， CoreWeave 的问题核⼼在于其⾃⾝的商业模

式，⽽⾮ AI 的整体前景，但他也坦承这是⼀个 " 硬币的两⾯ "—— 究竟是孤⽴的公司层⾯问题，还
是揭⽰了更⼴泛的结构性脆弱性，⽬前仍是 " 各占⼀半的概率 " 。
对投资者⽽⾔，当前的关键不是 CoreWeave 会不会倒，⽽是算⼒定价能否⽀撑起这个周期的融
资逻辑。这个问题的答案，将决定这场恐慌的最终归宿。

### Page 4

市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

## Document 283: DeepSeek全面开源推理框架：兼容英伟达、华为昇腾芯片，单集群日吞吐155万亿Token，每卡每小时收入38美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/DeepSeek全面开源推理框架：兼容英伟达、华为昇腾芯片，单集群日吞吐155万亿Token，每卡每小时收入38美元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

DeepSeek 全⾯开源推理框架 DeepInfer 及 Token 编解码组件 deepseek-recipe ，兼容英伟
达与华为昇腾平台。基于该框架， DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 单集群⽇吞吐达 155 万亿 Token ，
按⾕电价计算每卡每⼩时收⼊ 38 美元。通过 Prefill 与 Decode 分离、 KVCache 复⽤及投机
解码等技术， DeepSeek 旨在提升推理效率、降低算⼒成本与能源消耗。
摘要：
DeepSeek 进⼀步开放⼤模型推理基础设施。
继开源⼤模型、 TileLang 编译⼯具及⼀系列推理算⼦库后， DeepSeek 宣布全⾯开源内部推理框
架 DeepInfer ，并同步开源 Token 编解码组件 deepseek-recipe ，旨在帮助开发者和企业优化⼤规
模模型部署，降低 AI 服务的算⼒成本与能源消耗。
DeepSeek 介绍，基于 DeepInfer ，团队已实现 DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 的⾼吞吐、低延迟⼤规模

推理服务。⽬前，单⼀集群的服务吞吐量达到每天 155 万亿 Token ，按⾕电价计算，每卡每⼩时
收⼊为 38 美元。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

推理框架是连接⼤模型能⼒与终端⽤⼾的关键基础设施。随着 AI 应⽤调⽤量增⻓，推理服务的成
本不仅取决于模型本⾝，还受到计算、通信、任务调度及缓存管理等环节的影响。如何在满⾜
延迟要求的同时提⾼有效吞吐，已成为⼤规模 AI 服务降本增效的重要环节。
兼容英伟达与华为昇腾，针对不同推理阶段优化算⼒配置
DeepInfer 集成⾼性能算⼦与深度优化的推理引擎，⽀持分布式推理、推测解码、 SWA Bounded
Replay 及结构化输出等能⼒，可在英伟达与华为昇腾平台运⾏。未来， DeepSeek 新模型的推理
代码及后续性能优化也将在该框架中持续开源。
⾯对⼤规模 AI 调⽤需求， DeepSeek 将推理系统的优化重点放在吞吐量与延迟上。系统采⽤
Prefill 与 Decode 分离部署：前者负责处理输⼊提⽰词，后者负责逐步⽣成 Token 。由于两个阶段
的计算负载不同，分开部署可以独⽴配置资源，分别优化⾸个 Token ⽣成时间和后续 Token ⽣成
间隔。

### Page 3

在硬件组织上， DeepSeek 使⽤常⻅的 8 卡机器承担 Prefill 任务，并采⽤ 64 ⾄ 128 卡超节点承担
Decode 任务。同时，系统通过⾃动扩缩容动态调整两个阶段的实例⽐例，减少流量变化导致的
资源错配；在 MoE 模型推理中，则通过专家并⾏与动态负载均衡，降低不同计算卡之间的负载
差异。
KVCache 最⻓保留 7 天，减少重复计算开销
缓存复⽤是 DeepSeek 降低推理成本的另⼀项关键设计。随着⻓上下⽂对话和 AI Agent 应⽤增
多，相同前缀可能在不同请求中反复出现。如果每次都重新处理这些内容，就会产⽣额外的计
算开销。
DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 将 KVCache 分为 Block KVCache 和 SWA KVCache 两部分，采⽤不同的存
储策略。其中， Block KVCache 存储在独⽴的 3FS 分布式⽂件系统中，相关存储容量约为 100
PiB 。根据 DeepSeek 披露的数据， Agent 流量的 KVCache 保留时间达到 3 天，聊天机器⼈流量则

达到 7 天。
当 后 续 请 求 命 中 已 有 缓 存 时 ， 系 统 可 以 复 ⽤ 此 前 的 计 算 结 果 ， 减 少 重 复 的 Prefill 计 算 。
DeepSeek 披露， 3FS 存储集群的峰值聚合读取带宽达到 10 TiB/s ，峰值聚合写⼊带宽约为 850
GiB/s ，以满⾜⼤规模缓存读写需求。
对于主要在活跃会话内短期复⽤的 SWA KVCache ， DeepSeek 则选择保留在机器内存中，不进
⾏持久化存储。线上数据显⽰，这部分缓存的保留时间约为 180 秒，命中率超过 98% 。
针对 SWA KVCache 未命中的情况， DeepSeek 采⽤ SWA Bounded Replay 机制，仅重算最近
128 个 Token ，并将注意⼒窗⼝限制在重算区间内，以降低缓存缺失带来的额外计算成本。
投机解码提升吞吐，编解码组件同步开源
除缓存复⽤外， DeepSeek 还通过投机解码和异步调度提⾼推理效率。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
292 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
图⽚
DeepSeek 采⽤ DSpark 投机解码框架，根据候选 Token 的预期接受概率动态决定验证范围，避免
将验证算⼒浪费在低接受概率的候选结果上。 DeepSeek 披露，在线部署中，与固定验证 5 个
Token 的⽅式相⽐，变⻓验证使吞吐提升 6% 。
在调度环节，系统通过 CPU 与 GPU 之间的异步协作，让请求调度、前向计算和采样等操作尽可
能重叠，减少设备等待时间。此外， DeepInfer ⽀持结构化输出，可依据 JSON Schema 、正则
表达式等约束控制模型⽣成结果，满⾜特定应⽤对输出格式的要求。
DeepSeek 还同步开源⽣产级 Token 编解码组件 deepseek-recipe 。该组件⽀持 Chat Completions

API 、 Responses API 和 Messages API 等主流接⼝，并通过特殊 Token 边界识别、普通⽂本分段
并⾏编码及流式输出解析等机制，提升⻓⽂本处理效率，确保第三⽅部署的 API 处理逻辑与官⽅
服务保持⼀致。
不过， DeepSeek 并未开放推理系统的全部组件。由于 KVCache 管理和负载均衡部分与内部基础
设施耦合较深，相关代码⽬前尚未完整开源。
从模型、算⼦库到⼤规模推理框架， DeepSeek 此次进⼀步扩展了开源技术栈的覆盖范围。对于
开发者和企业⽽⾔，这有望降低构建私有化及公有云 AI 服务的技术⻔槛；对于⼤模型⾏业⽽⾔，
如何提⾼推理吞吐、复⽤计算结果并适配不同硬件平台，也正成为控制 AI 服务成本的重要⽅向。

## Document 292: El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

30 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EL NIÑO 2026–2027: MULTIPLE PRESSURES ON PRICES AND SUPPLIES
Hélène Drouot
The 2026–2027 El Niño climate event is expected to be one of the most intense in the last fifty years, with sea-surface-temperature
anomalies potentially exceeding +3°C in the equatorial Pacific. According to the World Meteorological Organisation (WMO), El Niño
is expected to persist until February 2027, with asymmetrical effects, as South-East Asia and northern Latin America will experience

droughts, while southern Brazil and Argentina could see heavy rainfall. Above all, El Niño is compounding the effects of global warming
and exacerbating existing extreme-weather events (droughts, floods and heatwaves). The economic effects of this climate shock mainly
play out through three avenues: agricultural commodity prices, energy and logistics (see heatmap).
Agricultural commodity prices: volatility is causing tensions, with inflationary effects

In India and South-East Asia, droughts will affect harvests, pushing up prices (particularly for coffee, cocoa, rice and palm oil). Conversely, in
southern Brazil and Argentina, heavy rainfall will boost soya and maize production, putting downward pressure on prices. According to the IMF,
El Niño causes global food prices to rise by between 5% (in a typical episode) and 8% (very high intensity) year-on-year, with the full impact being
felt 6 to 12 months after the climatic peak.

Therefore, for a given country, the weighting of food in its inflation index (CPI) plays a decisive role. As a result, all other things being equal, an 8%
rise in prices adds around 1 pp to inflation when food accounts for 13% of the CPI basket (average for countries shown in green on the heatmap),
1.8 pp when this share reaches 22% (yellow), 2.8 pp at 35% (orange) and nearly 4 pp above 49% (red).
The risks are skewed to the upside, as the 8% estimate does not take into account additional losses linked to droughts, floods and other extre -

me events, while policy responses to shocks (stockpiling by importers and increased export restrictions) may amplify and prolong price pres -
sures. The rice market between 2023 and 2024 illustrates this well, as the combination of rice-export restrictions imposed by India in July 2023
(in order to protect its domestic market) and of the drought in South-East Asia contributed to a 30% average increase in the price of rice over the
course of 2023 and kept global prices high for several months after the end of the El Niño episode.

Energy production sector: heavy reliance on hydroelectricity leaves the sector vulnerable to a second supply shock
Droughts will reduce hydroelectric power generation in Latin America and South-East Asia. As a result, some countries will have to turn to other,
more expensive solutions, such as fossil fuels, against a backdrop of already high oil and gas prices. This shift will increase global demand for
oil and put pressure on prices which, according to the IMF, could persist for up to four quarters after the initial El Niño shock. For example,

in Colombia, where hydropower accounts for 55% of electricity generation, a 30% drop in output (already seen in 2023–2024) could increase the
cost of electricity generation by 15 to 20% (assuming that the cost of thermal power generation is twice as high as hydropower). In Ecuador (where
hydropower accounts for 70% of electricity generation) and Vietnam (30%), electricity shortages are likely to disrupt local industries.
SOURCE: IMF, WORLD BANK, EMBER, BNP PARIBAS CALCULATIONS

Reading note: Scores are standardised on a scale of 0 to 10 for ease of comparison between countries and supply channels.
- Food vulnerability: based on the weight of food in the CPI.
- Energy vulnerability: based on the share of hydropower in electricity generation.
- Logistics vulnerability: the ‘Trade and transport-related infrastructure’ component of the World Bank’s Logistics Perfor-

mance Index, which measures the quality of the infrastructure required for transport and international trade.

2
The bank for

Chart of the Week / 30 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
Logistics: transport infrastructure is another point of vulnerability
The final channel relates to disruptions to freight transport against a backdrop of already weakened value chains. The variable used in our
heatmap is a proxy for economies’ abilities to absorb a shock (particularly a climate-related one) affecting their transport infrastructure (for

example, by maintaining traffic flow, diverting or reorganising transport flows). The heatmap highlights the particularly high vulnerability
of Latin America. Its capacity to absorb a logistics shock is relatively limited, and there is significant exposure at a strategic chokepoint, the
Panama Canal, through which approximately 5% of global trade passes. Low rainfall in the canal basin has already led to the number of
daily transits being reduced in September 2026 (as in 2023–2024); further restrictions are expected in October (to 29 transits per day, down

from 36 in August), which will increase transport times and costs. Europe is also being affected, with low water levels on the Rhine and the
Danube already disrupting river transport and electricity generation.
Latin America and South-East Asia are hit hardest
Therefore, the economies most at risk are not necessarily those that will suffer the greatest climate shock, but those where multiple trans -
mission channels converge, including a high weighting of food prices in inflation, reliance on hydroelectricity, fragile transport infrastructure

and limited fiscal room for manoeuvre. Latin America and South-East Asia are home to some of the most vulnerable countries, particularly
due to their dependence on food prices and hydroelectricity. Colombia and Ecuador are noteworthy for their high exposure to energy risk,
while Ghana, India and the Philippines are some of the economies most vulnerable to a food shock. Latin America faces a combination of
vulnerabilities, such as high exposure to food and energy price shocks in some countries, which are compounded by logistics risks, as currently

illustrated by the restrictions on traffic through the Panama Canal.
helene.drouot@bnpparibas.com

Raissi
Callahan, C. W., & Mankin, J. S. (2023). Persistent economic impacts of El Niño events. Science Advances, 9(20)
World Meteorological Organisation. (2026). “El Niño is expected to reach very strong intensity, increasing the risk of extreme weather events

until 2027“

Oct Edition: Choppy Markets, Intact AI

Overseas  
Technology

Jeff Pu, CFA 
SFC CE No. BNO719 
jeffpu@gfgroup.com.hk 
Henry Huang 
SFC CE No. BYA749 
henryhuang@gfgroup.com.hk

Market to remain choppy: Tech equities were volatile in the past month, with rotation 
toward hyperscaler and internet names. That said , s emi and memory (Korea) 
weakness reflected lower risk appetite due to rising bond yields, AI monetization/ARR 
visibility and L-T AI debates (i.e., Broadcom CEO warned that capital may now be a 
bigger constraint), even as company-specific catalysts continued to be strong. On the 
other hand, the positive was the acceleration of AI model scaling (i.e., Meta’s Muse 
Wonder, Gemini 4, etc). That said, w e view the pullback primarily as a reset in 
expectations rather than the evidence of AI deterioration. Despite light positioning, we 
expect the markets will remain choppy in the near term but would  accumulate low 
valuation & sustainable names  (i.e., HBM, non-TSMC foundries, CPUs, CPOs, etc) . 
In our preferred list, we added CLS, while removing MTK and Hon Hai.  
U.S. roadshow takeaways (week of Oct 5): Investor tone skewed neutral to slightly 
cautious, driven by conservative top ‑down macro views, U.S. election, and ongoing 
debates around the L-T AI demand. Positioning in AI infrastructure has come off peak 
levels. Even  so, many investors would accumulate on pullbacks, with interest 
concentrated in “Made in U.S.” names (INTC, LITE, COHR), U.S. memory suppliers, 
TSMC, and selective bottleneck themes — including ASE, ABF substrates, 
MRVL/SMTC, and Japanese MLCCs. 
OCP & earnings season in focus: The week is anchored by AI/semis — SEMICON 
West, OCP, and earnings from ASML, TSMC, etc. OCP reinforced the industry’s pivot 
toward rack‑scale infrastructure, with inference and agentic workloads driving demand 
across compute, memory, and networking. Scale‑up optics, NPO/CPO, and 800VDC 
power were key themes, while AMD’s CPU outlook and Helios/Helion underscored a 
secular CPU uptrend. Nvidia ’s use of PTFE for NVLink boards further supports our 
positive stance on PTFE (see PCB Spec in 2 H27, Oct 5).  On earnings, ASML 
delivered largely in -line results, GM and OP above consensus with revenue slightly 
miss, and raised full year guidance again and is exploring ways to increase capacity 
by >30% 2028 to fulfill strong customer demand, also extending the order visibility 
beyond 2028. For TSMC, we anticipate an inline quarter. 
September monthly sales/data: TSMC sales -0.6% MoM, slightly ahead. Rubin to 
accelerate and T3 ramp. PC forecast trimmed. Substrate/CCL upcycle with 
pricing/margin expansion. Aspeed revenue +101% YoY. Details on page 5. 
GFHK Tech Most Read:  1) Jeff Pu on ASIC Updates on TPU v9 ; 2) Michelle Jing 
on PCB Spec in 2H27; 3) Alicia Xia on CPO Acceleration; 4) Jeff Pu on NAND Re-
acceleration; 5) Jeff Pu on TSMC 3Q26 Preview.

Sector Report | Technology

GFHK Tech Important Stock Calls  
In the past one month, our key reports and stock calls are: 
Jeff Pu on ASIC Updates on TPU v9, reported date on Oct 13: We expect near-term MTK headwinds 
from TPU v9 delays but remain constructive on MTK/MRVL longer term. Humufish’s redesign could 
push tape-out to Jan -27, MP tape -out to 2Q27 and shipments to 1Q28, while EMIB -T yields could 
recover from 45–50%. Rising TPU allocations to Anthropic could constrain Gemini compute, though 
higher internal allocation in 2H26 may support future models. SemiAnalysis expects Anthropic/OpenAI 
to each add 5 –10GW in 2027 vs. a few GW for Google. For TPU v10 (4Q28 /1Q29), MTK/GUC’s 
Icefish remains the only confirmed mainstream SKU, while Broadcom-led Merope faces AMD/Marvell 
competition, with both potentially testing EMIB-T. 
Michelle Jing on PCB Spec in 2H27, reported date on Oct 5: Some market watchers expect Nvidia 
to shift from PTFE to hydrocarbon resin (HC) for the VR300 NVL576 switch tray. HC—one of the glass 
free alternatives within PTFE hybrid architectures—has only recently passed SI testing and has yet to 
enter PCB MP, leaving it roughly three months behind PTFE in qualification. On the contrary, we 
believe that PTFE offers superior performance for high frequency, high speed designs. With VR300 
NVL576 shipments expected in 2H27 and the final configuration likely set by end 26 —and with a 
leading Chinese PCB ma ker already achieving meaningful PTFE hybrid yields —we maintain that 
PTFE hybrid remains the most probable solution for next year’s switch board. 
Alicia Xia on CPO Acceleration , reported date on  Sept 29 : Nvidia’s CPO scale -out switch is 
progressing faster than expected, with FAU yields improving. We forecast CPO switch shipments at 
15k/16k/30k in 4Q26/1Q27/2Q27 and 100k in 2027. For Nvidia/AWS scale -up, CPO and NPO 
continue in parallel, with full NPO adoption expected at NVL576 launch. Feynman is also devel oping 
both variants, with CPO potentially using QM6 + six 6.4T OEs. We shift AWS Trainium 4 launch to 
1Q28 and forecast global scale-up OE demand at 6.5m/42m in 2027E/28E. 
Jeff Pu on NAND Re -acceleration, reported date on Sept 20 : According to TrendForce, NAND 
demand is stronger than expected as U.S. CSPs scale AI infrastructure, with eSSD demand nearly 
2x prior forecasts. We raise NAND contract price growth to +21%/+17%/+15% QoQ in 
3Q26/4Q26/1Q27, while hyperscaler inventories decline. Supply remains disci plined at 15%/21%/20% 
YoY bit growth in 2025 –27. Demand is supported by agentic AI, vector databases and KV -cache 
offloading, where eSSD offers lower cost/bit than DRAM and better power efficiency than HDD. 
Jeff Pu on TSMC 3Q26 Preview , reported date on  Oct 7 : TSMC shares hit record highs on 
expectations ahead of the print and potential Terafab collaboration. We expect largely inline 3Q26 
results, capex and full-year guidance, with softer 4Q26 margin (67.2%). Management should reaffirm 
2026 capex at the ceiling and mid-40s YoY growth, while we forecast 37% growth in 2027 on sustained 
AI/CPU/networking demand and price hikes. Key focuses include margins, long -term capex/U.S. 
expansion, large-reticle packaging and A14. We raise 2026E/27E EPS by 2%/8% on hig her N2/N3 
capacity and operating leverage, and roll to 20x 2027E P/E, lifting TP to NT$2,960.

### Page 5

Sector Report | Technology

September Monthly Data  
TSMC September sales of NT$511.9bn, -0.6% MoM and +54.6% YoY, with total 3Q revenue reaching 
NT$1.49T, +17.6 QoQ, slightly ahead of expectations of +14%. The solid performance was well 
supported by ASIC, A20 Pro, and Rubin. For 3Q26, we expect gross margin to be 67.8%, higher than 
guidance of 65-67% and consensus of 66.4% . For 4Q26, we expect another 12% QoQ growth with 
GM at 67.2%, both ahead of street’s estimates of +10% QoQ and 66% margin. Ahead of the earnings 
call, key focus include gross margin trajectory, long -term capex discipline, and the execution of its 
U.S. fab expansion. 
Rubin to accelerate:  Hon Hai revenue last month was NT$1.16T, +26 % MoM, +38% YoY, mainly 
driven by iPhone build.  Quanta and Wistron delivered -23% and -17% MoM respectively, due to AI 
racks’ shipping timing differences. Of note, Pegatron surprised the market with +143.2% MoM revenue 
surge fueled by initial GB300 rack shipments to neocloud customers including Lambda and Together 
AI. This reaffirms our thesis of robust NVL72 order momentum across Neoclouds. Looking ahead, 
VR200 is ramping now, with volume deployment accelerating into 2027. We maintain our forecast of 
75k and 92k NVL72 (GB+VR) rack shipments for 2026 and 2027, respectively. Concurrently, Wiwynn 
delivered another solid month with revenue expanding 20.0% MoM, underpinne d by resilient GP 
server demand alongside the ramp of Trainium 3. 
ASIC ramping up: On design service vendors fronts, driven  by T3 ramp, Alchip’s Sep revenue 
reached a record NT$9.9 8bn. Backed by secured incremental 3nm wafer allocation and improving 
yields, management raised its 2027 revenue guidance, now targeting growth above the previous 50% 
YoY baseline. That said, we push out T4 shipment timeline into 1Q28. GUC delivered a strong beat 
with monthly revenue reaching a record NT$7.145bn (+22.4% MoM, +97.8 % YoY), driven by robust 
Axion 2 CPU demand. Beyond its engagement with top 4 CSPs, multi -year order visibility into 2027 
and beyond is further bolstered by high-margin (likely TK1) automotive ASIC designs. 
PC forecast trimmed: NB shipments from top 5 ODMs were 9.7m in Sep, with 3Q26 shipments down 
17% QoQ and 26% YoY. Global PC OEM shipments contracted 20.1% YoY in 3Q26, worse than 
expected, post robust 1H26 pull -ins with high pricing points and weak macro. For 2027, we project 
the industry will decline a further 5% YoY. On the competitive front, tier -1 vendors such as Lenovo, 
Dell, and HP are not immune to this downturn, while entry-level laptops (low cost models from China) 
continue to see stronger relative demand.  
Substrate/CCL Upcycle Intact: Unimicron continued to deliver another all-time high sales with +5% 
MoM and 65% YoY. Supported by ABF upward pricing of ~15% in 2H26 (likely more next year) and 
higher mix of AI products, we expect a meaningful GM uptick for this quarter with momentum to sustain 
into 2027 . In the meantime, ZengDing’s revenue rose 28% MoM and 36% YoY mainly driven by  
iPhone, 1.6T mSAP, and Rubin’s HDI. On the CCL front, bolstered by ongoing price hikes and strong 
demand from GP servers, ITEC revenue grew 15.5% MoM and 111% YoY, and NanYa Plastics 
recorded +12.5% MoM and +55% YoY. Meanwhile, EMC and TUC both posted another single digit 
growth MoM on sales.

Downstream tech – Smartphone & Apple  
We maintain Global smartphone forecast of -14% in 2026  
According to IDC, the preliminary 2Q26 smartphone data points to a still ‑soft market with uneven 
regional dynamics, and their commentary continues to emphasize structural rather than cyclical 
headwinds. Smartphone shipments remain under pressure from weak replacement cycles, cautious 
consumer spending, and limited breakthrough features. IDC typically highlights that “growth is 
concentrated in the premium tier,” driven by ecosystem lock ‑in, while the mass market remains 
challenged by price sensitivity. They also tend to note that China and parts of emerging markets show 
only modest stabilization, not a full recovery, and that vendors are prioritizing margin discipline over 
volume.  
Overall, by factoring in higher iPhone 17/17 Pro volume, we fine tune our global smartphone shipment 
forecast to -14% YoY from -15% YoY. 
For 2027, we expect global smartphone shipments to be flattish YoY. That said, 2026 -2027 will be 
one of the most challenging periods for the industry, driven primarily by sharply higher memory prices, 
which have already pushed OEMs to reduce shipments and raise device prices.

iPhone 18 Pro/18 PM demand lukewarm  
iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max wait-time lukewarm: iPhone 18 Pro and iPhone 18 Pro Max pre-orders began 
on Sept 12 in the U.S. and many other countries. Generally speaking, the wait-time appears lukewarm, 
with: 1) 3–4 weeks for 256GB/512GB Pro Max across all colors, as well as Burgundy 1TB in the U.S.；
2) 2–3 weeks for Glacier 256GB/512GB Pro Max in China; 3) 1–2 weeks for Black/Glacier 1TB Pro 
Max in the U.S. ；4) immediate availability for Silver 1TB and mos t 2TB Pro Max models in the U.S, 
as well as most iPhone 18 Pro configurations except for Burgundy 256GB in the U.S. We attribute the 
lukewarm wait-time to limited spec upgrade, higher pricing (especially for large storage models), etc. 
We also expect the Pro Max to be impacted by iPhone Duo and the criticism of heavy weight.  
iPhone Duo feedback generally positive 
The iPhone Duo, priced at $1,999 in the U.S., opened pre‑orders on Oct 16 and officially launched on 
Oct 23. Early reviews were positive, highlig hting its polished folding experience, high ‑quality inner 
display, and improved multitasking in iOS 27, but also point to trade ‑offs including a heavier and 
thicker form factor, a visible crease, and a dual ‑camera. Due to hinge constraints, we are reducing  
the iPhone Duo EMS build plan from 7m to 6m units in 2026. We also lower the iPhone 18 Pro/Pro 
Max build plan to 72m units given the constraints associated with the adjustable‑aperture camera.

Sector Report | Technology

Downstream tech – PC 
Global PC units down to -12% in 2026, followed by another decline in 2027 
On Oct 8, IDC reported 3Q26 global PC shipments fell 20.1% YoY, missing street expectations of a 
10–15% decline. The sharper contraction was primarily driven by front -loaded demand in 1H26, 
elevated ASPs, and broader macro weakness. This aligns with our prior view that ODM build 
momentum moderated starting in end -2Q26, amid the rising concerns over channel inventory. 
Meanwhile, the top 5 ODMs also saw 3Q26 shipments decline 26% YoY. Looking ahead, we model 
4Q26 shipments to continue 20% YoY drop (flattish QoQ), underperforming normal seasonality. 
On the component front, we expect memory de -spec to normalize. End -users still require sufficient 
DRAM capacity (16GB+) to handle on -device AI workloads, and PC DDR 5 supply constraints have 
eased compared to Server’s. On pricing, new models will still have higher pricing points to pass 
through 2H component cost increases across memory, CPU, etc. That said, we anticipate price hikes 
will remain measured vs 1H26 given sluggish end-market demand. 
Overall, reflecting weaker than expected 3Q26 results post pull -ins and a muted 4Q outlook, we trim 
our 2026 PC shipment forecast to -12% YoY. For 2027, we project shipments to decline further by 5% 
YoY, as higher ASP and persistent macro headwinds continue to weigh on demand.

Upstream tech – AI Semi & Supply Chain  
U.S. CSP capex to be +85%/+53%/+20% in 2026/2027/2028 
Given capex expands further into 2027, CSPs free cash flow are undergoing significant pressure and 
turning negative except Microsoft, such that shifting to debt financing for their massive capex. In 2025, 
Top 5 CSPs’ total debt issuance reached $108bn, or 24% of total capex . Per our Addressing 2028 
Capex and AI Platform Value Shift report on Aug 6, we forecast Top 5 CSPs’ debt issuance to reach 
$257bn/$419bn in 2026/2027, or 31%/35% of total capex.  Assuming debt issuance to grow in 
2028/2029 at a mild pace, we believe U.S. CSPs' balance sheet (except Oracle) to remain very healthy, 
due to net leverage (EBITDA basis) of only 0.5 and the net debt-to-equity ratio of only 18% by 2029.  
Despite healthy balance sheet for most CSPs, we expect most to safeguard balance sheets beyond 
2027 amid widening spreads and FCF profiles, with the focus shifting to returns. Together with a swing 
factor of competition from Chinese LLMs, we expect U.S. CSPs to decelerate capex expansion. We 
forecast Top 5 CSPs capex of $1,512bn/1,739bn in 2028/2029, or +20%/15% YoY, down from +53% 
in 2027.

Server CPU TAM to grow at 49% CAGR by 2030 
As the orchestration core of AI infrastructure buildouts, the Server CPU has gained significant traction 
over the past few months. This is driven by the Agentic AI/inference trend, fueled by the breakthrough 
of OpenClaw and Anthropic’s ARR surging to $44bn  in April. For instance, in latest earnings calls, 
AMD projected a >35% CAGR for Server CPU TAM by 2030, and Intel said the shift toward agentic 
AI is driving a structural demand for CPU, where the GPU-to-CPU ratio is tightening from 8:1 in training 
to 4:1 in inference. Based on our estimates, assuming inference accounts for 90% of AI workloads 
and GPU-to-CPU ratio for AI servers to reach 1:1.2 in 2030, we now expect Server CPU TAM to grow 
96%/71% in 2026/2027, reaching $215bn by 2030 from $30bn in 2025, with a 5-yr CAGR of 49%.

According to AMD’s Advancing AI Event on July 23, AMD raised TAM for server CPU: The company

Upstream tech – Optical 
Strong demand for pluggable through 2028. 
We expect that the total demand for 800G/1.6T will reach 90mn/80mn in 2027, increased by 
80%/167%+, mainly driven by: 1) Nvidia, Google and AWS and other ASIC’s Accelerators’ demand 
are ramping up; 2) GPU/ASIC scale -out bandwidth continuously  increase drives increasing 
GPU/ASIC-to-optical module ratio; 3) We expect that TPU for 2027 will be 8.9m and Google fully 
transitions to large -scale clusters in 2026 and the scale -up layer will adopt optical interconnects. In 
addition, as we expect that CPO/NPO will start since 2H27, represents a pure incremental opportunity 
for optical interconnects supply chain, benefiting FAU, laser, shuffle box, connector and OE players, 
including LITE, GLW, TSEM, Sumitomo and Browave. We also like Marvell and SMTC, for the 
TIA/Driver used in NPO OE.  
Scale-out bandwidth increase driving optical-module deployment 
For NVIDIA, each Rubin GPU is equipped with two CX9 NIC chips, doubling the scale-out bandwidth 
compared with Blackwell. We further estimate that Rubin Ultra will adopt four CX9 chips per GPU, 
leading to another 2 times increase in scale-out bandwidth, continuing NVIDIA’s consistent bandwidth-
doubling trajectory. Accordingly, we expect the ratio of 1.6T optical modules has risen from 1:2.5 to 
1:5 in Rubin. From CoWoS perspective, we expect 5m/2m Blackwell/Rubin and 20m 1.6T in 2026. 
For Google, we believe that Goo gle fully transition to large -scale clusters in 2026 and the scale -up 
layer will adopt optical interconnects. We believe that in the scale-up domain, the ratio of TPU to 1.6T 
optical modules is approximately 1:1.5; In the scale-out domain, the ratio is around 1:2.5, suggesting 
the overall TPU-to-optics ratio is approximately 1:4 (1.6T equivalent).

Nvidia/CSPs’ Scale-out/up CPO/NPO  
Within NVIDIA’s next -gen NVL576 and broader CSPs’ AI infrastructure plans, CPO and NPO are 
developed side -by-side to expand GP U/ASIC interconnect bandwidth: CPO is the ideal long -term 
design for lower power and latency yet faces headwinds from complicated advanced packaging, and 
yield risks, while NPO could deliver most short -reach optical performance gains with far better 
manufacturability and deployment flexibility, making NPO a reliable near -term scaling path and CPO 
the ultimate end -state architecture. NPO partially removes module -side DSP and shifts signal 
conditioning and equalization to system designs, greatly lifting the value of TIAs and laser drivers that 
take over part of traditional DSP functions; we estimate each 3.2T optical engine contains tens of 
dollars worth of combined TIA and driver chips, a clear improvement over standard pluggable optics. 
Our shipment forecasts at 6.5m/42.4m NPO optical engines in CY27/28.  
Scale-up domain to increase by NVL576 to compensate lower memory: With lower memory spec, we 
expect Nvidia to increase the scale -up domain by optics to enhance performance. According to 
Nvidia’s GTC 2026, th e optical scale -up fabric allows Nvidia to expand Rubin Ultra to an NVL576 
scale-up. Regarding CPO and NPO which are being parallelly developed, we expect NPO to be a 
high reliability scaling path for NPO. We now expect its scale -up to adopt NPO in Rubin U ltra and 
Feynman platform and estimate OE shipments of Nvidia’s platform to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028.

### Page 25

Sector Report | Technology

Nvidia’s NPO OE to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028 
For Nvidia, we now expect its scale up to adopt NPO in Rubin Ultra and Feynman platform and 
estimate OE  shipments of Nvidia’s platform to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028, based on full NPO 
adoption at NVL576 launch given NPO maturity. Coupled with other CSP’s platform, we estimate total 
OE market to be 6.5m/42.4m in 2027/2028.  
TIA/Driver to benefit from NPO 
NPO partially removes module -side DSP and shifts signal conditioning and equalization to system 
designs, greatly lifting the value of TIAs and laser drivers that take over part of traditional DSP 
functions; we estimate each 3.2T optical engine contains tens o f dollars worth of combined TIA and 
driver chips, a clear improvement over standard pluggable optics.  
A key architectural shift under NPO is the partial removal of DSP functionality at the module level, with 
signal conditioning and equalization increasing ly redistributed into system -level design. This 
significantly elevates the importance of TIA (transimpedance amplifier) and laser driver components, 
which effectively replace a portion of traditional DSP value. We estimate that each 3.2T optical engine 
will embed high tens of dollars of combined TIA and driver content, representing a meaningful step -
up versus traditional pluggable optics architectures.  
From a competitive standpoint, we expect Marvell to remain the primary beneficiary of both NPO OE 
given i ts stronger positioning high speed TIA/Driver. Semtech is well positioned as a secondary 
beneficiary through its exposure to high-speed analog signal chain components, particularly TIAs and 
laser drivers, which scale directly with optical engine proliferat ion under NPO. We forecast 
6.5mn/42.4mn NPO OE shipments in CY27/28. Based on our estimated front-end content per OE and 
a 30% market share assumption for Semtech, we estimate its NPO revenue of $315/936mn. 
Stock Implication 
Generally speaking, scale-up CPO/NPO is an incremental opportunity for optical interconnect supply 
chain, as it aims to replace copper and has no impact on scale -out side. Additionally, scale up and 
scale out CPO solution share the similar suppliers. That said, we expect the key benefic iaries to be 
components such as FAU, CW laser, ELSFP, shuffle, PIC, TIA and Driver. We: 1) prefer Lumentum/ 
COHR due to the demand upside for CW laser, and also like local cw laser players; 2) expect Browave 
(3163 TT) to benefit, given the sizable contribu tion of CPO’s shuffle box considering ~55% market 
share, $5-6k ASP and a volume ramp in 2H26; 3) we also like TSEM, given CSP preferring NPO 
solution for their ASICs scale up interconnection. 4) expect GLW to benefit, given the second supplier 
of shuffle b ox and main supplier of MMC connector/fiber. 5) Marvell/Semtech will also benefit from 
CSP/Nvidia’s NPO solution, since TIA and driver will upgrade in the NPO Optical Engine.

Sector Report | Technology

Google’s 6D Torus & Beneficiaries.  
We expect Google will migrate its TPU interconnect from 3D to 6D torus, re -organizing the 64 -chip 
elemental block from 4× 4× 4 to 2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2. External optical links per block rise from 96 to 384, with 
optical ports per chip scaling increase from 1:1.5 to 1:6, driving 4x coherent lite transceivers and OCS 
demand. The trajectory aligns with NVIDIA's CPO/NPO move. Both are interconnect scaling that 
expands direct chip-to-chip bandwidth and reduces hop count, with TPU via a 6D torus and NVIDIA 
via CPO/NPO for Scale-up. 
Google’s migration from 3D  to 6D Torus: We expect Google to migrate its TPU interconnect from a 
3D to a 6D torus topology, re-organizing the elemental 64-chip building block from a 4× 4× 4 3D Torus 
cube into a 2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2 hyper-cube. In the current 3D design, each chip connects to 6 nearest 
neighbors across X/Y/Z axes and the cube exposes 16 optical ICI links on each of its 6 faces, totaling 
96 external optical links per cube (~1.5 optical ports per chip). Under the 6D design, each dimension 
has 64 external optical links (2⁶ chips, on e outward link per chip per dimension), for a total of 6 ×  64 
= 384 external optical links (~6 optical ports per chip). The per-chip optical port count thus scales from 
1.5 to 6. Combined with Scale -out at an estimated 1:2.5, this brings the aggregate TPU -to-port ratio 
to 1:8.5. 
Lower latency and closer to non -blocking Scale-up: For a 4,096 -chip superpod, a 3D torus (4× 4× 4) 
carries a worst-case diameter of 24 hops, whereas a 6D torus (2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2) halves that to 12 hops 
(2× 6), directly compressing collective-operation and latency. Higher dimensionality also spreads traffic 
across 6 orthogonal paths, improving bisection bandwidth and moving closer to non-blocking chip-to-
chip communication. Critically, shortening each edge from 4 to 2 nodes eliminates deep mu lti-hop 
electrical paths inside the block: the architecture deliberately reduces intra -cube copper 
communication and pushes a far larger share of traffic onto optical fiber. 
Key beneficiaries: The exact TPU generation for the adoption of 6D torus and initi al penetration rate 
remain unclear. That said, whether NVIDIA moves toward CPO/NPO or Google expands from 3D to 
6D, both are Scale -up strategies aimed at enabling greater inter -chip communication. Key 
beneficiaries include the 2.4T Coherent Lite module and the OCS supply chain, such as MEMS-based 
optical switch vendors, PIC/laser/DSP suppliers, and fiber/cabling vendors supporting the larger 
reconfigurable lightwave fabric.

Risks 
1) Alternative technologies; 2) AI demand and capex slowdown; 3) IT demand slowdown; 4) product 
delays.

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

### Page 2

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Stopping for gas
• We estimate that New Zealand consumer
prices rose by 0.7% in the September quarter,
with a moderation in fuel costs balanced
against increases in council rates, building
costs and food prices.
• The annual inflation rate is expected to drop
back to 3.7% (down from 4.1% in the year
to  June).
• Measures of core inflation are expected to
remain firm, with some closely watched
measures expected to rise to the top of the

RBNZ’s target band.
• Our forecast is a little below the RBNZ’s last
published forecast for 3.9% annual inflation.

We expect the September quarter inflation report (out
on 22 October) will show that New Zealand consumer
prices rose 0.7% over the past three months. That would
see the annual inflation rate slipping back to 3.7%, down
from 4.1% in the year to June. However, as we discuss

in our recent forecast update, that’s likely to be just a
temporary reprieve with the recent rise in oil prices set to
push inflation up again over the coming months.
16 Oct 2026 | Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5668 | +64 21 710 852 | satish.ranchhod@westpac.co.nz
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
CPI preview, September quarter 2026 –
Thursday 22 October, 10:45am.

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Key drivers of quarterly inflation.
Underpinning our forecast for 0.7% inflation in the
September quarter are some big price movements in
both directions.
The largest move in prices this quarter has actually been
a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by any
stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for
both petrol and diesel over the September quarter were
lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed

higher in the wake of the Middle East conflict – see the
chart below. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91
unleaded was around 6% lower in September than in the
June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower.
Fuel spending accounts for around 4% of the CPI, and
these moves have taken 0.4ppts off inflation this quarter.

While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in
the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully high. And
that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other
goods and services. Of note, the September quarter
saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with
international airfares up 14% and domestic airfares up

nearly 8%. Together those prices account for around 2%
of the CPI.
We also expect building costs (10% of the CPI) will rise by
1.6% this quarter following a similarly chunky rise in June.
Again, much of that rise has been related to higher global
oil prices which have been flowing through to higher
operating and material costs. That’s occurred at the same
time as the amount of planned construction work has
been rising.
The September quarter also sees the annual increases

in local council rates and charges for services like water
supply (around 4% of the CPI). We expect these costs will
be up around 7.4% this year, continuing the run of large
increases seen in previous years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices
(18% of the CPI), largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
the price for fresh vegetables.
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in
housing rents, which are the largest component of

the CPI, accounting for 10% of the basket. With low
population growth and ample supply, rents have
effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual
terms, rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more
than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Where could we be surprised?
There are some key areas where we see the risk
of a downside surprise. The first is the price for
communications equipment, like mobile phones. After
a large rise in the June quarter, we could see a sizeable

drop in prices this quarter.
There could also be softness in used car prices, especially
with high fuel prices encouraging a shift away from
combustion engine vehicles.
However, the risks aren’t all to one side. Demand for more
environmentally friendly new cars has picked up, and that
could push up prices for those vehicles. In addition, we’re
watching to see if high fuel costs are spilling over into
other prices as we’re seeing in sectors like construction.

Core inflation around 3%, not easing.
With big swings in fuel costs and concerns that this is
spilling over into a broader lift in inflation pressures,
the RBNZ will be paying close attention to the various
measures of core inflation (core inflation measures
smooth through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual
prices and instead track the underlying trend in inflation).
We expect that core inflation measures will point to
continued firmness in underlying price pressures, with

several closely watched measures likely to take a step
higher this quarter. In terms of specifics, we expect:
• CPI ex-fuel inflation: +3.0% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
• CPI ex-fuel and food: +3.2% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
• CPI ex-fuel, food and household energy: +2.9% yr (vs
+2.5% previously)

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
We’ll also be watching the distribution of price
changes to see if there’s been a material increase in
the number of prices that are increasing. That could
be an early indication that businesses, even those
with a limited exposure to fuel costs, are starting to
factor in expectations of continued high inflation when
setting prices.
As we’ve noted before, the various measures of core
inflation were not low even before the recent fuel shock.

That means the RBNZ has limited headroom to absorb
upside inflation surprises.
Much of that firmness in core inflation has been due
to increases in domestically oriented non-tradables
prices. We estimate these prices rose by 1.3% over the
September quarter. That would see annual non-tradables
inflation rising to 3.6%, up from 3.4% last quarter.
Non-tradables inflation is a key focus for the RBNZ and
it’s been running at these above-average levels for around

a year now. That’s despite the softness in economic
growth and the labour market. In part, this firmness in
domestic inflation is due to continued large increases in
administered prices, like council rates.
On the imported front, lower fuel costs are expected to
see tradables prices falling by 0.2% over the quarter.
That would result in annual tradables inflation dropping
back to 3.9% from 4.9% previously. Excluding food and
fuel costs, we estimate that tradables prices were up

2.2% over the past year (vs 1.9% in the year to June). That
would be the highest level in three years with the fall in
the NZD boosting import costs.

How do our forecasts compare to the
RBNZ’s assumptions?
The RBNZ’s last published forecast from their September

policy statement assumed that consumer prices would
rise by 0.8%qtr (3.9%yr). That’s slightly higher than we’re
expecting. Most of the difference is because the RBNZ is
expecting higher imported inflation.
A result in line with our forecast would give the RBNZ a
little more breathing room ahead of their upcoming policy
review on 28 October. We’re expecting they will leave the
OCR on hold at this meeting, while markets are pricing in
a roughly 66% chance of a 25bp hike.

However, even if the overall inflation result is close to
forecast, the underlying detail will be important. The
RBNZ will be watching closely for signs that high fuel
prices are spilling over into other prices, especially with
global oil prices taking another step higher recently. With
that in mind, the strength of the various core inflation
measures will be a key focus for the RBNZ.
Furthermore, while annual inflation is expected to
soften in the September quarter, this is likely to be just a

temporary reprieve. With a sharp rise in fuel costs over
the past few weeks, inflation is set to reaccelerate back
over 4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain
above the RBNZ’s forecast for most of next year.
September monthly prices.
Our forecast for a 0.7% rise in consumer prices in the
September quarter incorporates information from
Stats NZ’s latest monthly prices update. That update
did reveal some softer than expected food price

movements in September. However, that was balanced
against larger than expected rises in airfares and holiday
accommodation costs.

## Document 407: What sector analysis tells us about Europe's recovery, two years after the _Draghi_ report (2026-10-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/What sector analysis tells us about Europe's recovery, two years after the _Draghi_ report (2026-10-12).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026EDITORIAL
WHAT SECTOR ANALYSIS TELLS US ABOUT EUROPE'S RECOVERY, TWO YEARS AFTER THE "DRAGHI" REPORT
1 That amounts to EUR 800 billion to be committed in the EU by the end of the decade, which would bring defence budgets to 3.5% of GDP , up from 2.4% of GDP in
2026 (estimated figure). See Colliac, S. (2026), “Readiness 2030: One Year After Its Launch, the European Rearmament Plan Is on Track,” BNP Paribas EcoWeek, March.

2 Since 2025, critical metals have also been the focus of “strategic projects” for extraction in Europe, with the European Commission having just launched an ambi-
tious second wave, targeting lithium and cobalt in particular. See: Commission Selects 46 New Strategic Projects to Bolster the EU’s Supply of Critical Raw Materials
3 See Derrien G. (2026), “Next Generation EU: The Program Is Nearing Its End, but Its Impact on Economic Activity Will Endure,” BNP Paribas Chart of the Week, Sep-

tember 23.
4 NextGenerationEU Shows Strong Delivery Record as Implementation Reaches the Finish Line, October 7, 2026.
In 2026, the European economy will not perform better than it did in 2025. At 1.4% (IMF estimate), its growth remains half
as fast as in the United States. Still, there are more and more signs that Europe is regaining strength. The energy crisis,
which has now been ongoing for eight months, would have caused it to halt in other times. Beneath an apparent sluggish

activity, productivity is rebounding, while many sectors are picking up steam.
Insufficient growth and innovation, a technological lag, and an ex -
cessive dependence on China and the United States: two years ago,
the “Draghi” report painted a troubling picture of Europe and laid the
groundwork for a massive effort to revive the economy. Almost im -
mediately afterwards, the European Union (EU) adopted a “competi -
tiveness compass,” with the primary aim of strengthening its strategic

autonomy.
A 3D OUTLOOK: DEFENCE, DIGITALISATION, DECARBONISATION
Unsurprisingly given the geopolitical context, the most significant ini -
tiatives focused on defence: since March 2025, the SAFE (Security Ac -
tion for Europe) instrument has enabled the Commission to raise funds
on the markets (up to EUR 150 billion) to support member states in
implementing the “Readiness 2030” rearmament programme 1. Here,
the planning stage has long been surpassed, and disbursements for

the acquisition of military equipment are accelerating. According to the
EDA (European Defense Agency), European defence spending is projec-
ted to reach EUR 454 billion in 2026, already 30% more than in 2024.
The EU-27 have also set out to catch up in the field of artificial intel -
ligence (AI), committing up to EUR 200 billion in investments through
public-private partnerships. From fostering a culture of AI to promoting
and retaining talents, supporting startups, and sharing experiences and

data, the initiative is designed to be comprehensive. Its cornerstone is a
dedicated EUR 20 billion fund, co-financed by the Commission and the
European Investment Bank, which aims to establish five “giga-facto -
ries” across Europe capable of hosting and training the most powerful
AI models.
The EU is also leading the counteroffensive on other fronts, such as the
supply of critical metals, which it is seeking to secure through trade
agreements (most recently with Australia and South Africa) 2, and the

accelerated decarbonisation of the economy (with specific support pro-
vided to energy-intensive sectors). Its “Clean Industry Pact” is expected
to receive up to EUR 100 billion in funding from the Innovation Fund or
the European Union Emissions Trading System.
BENEATH THE SURFACE WINDS, SUPPORTIVE CURRENTS
In a report dated June 2026, the Institut Montaigne estimated that
nearly one-third of the recommendations in the “Draghi” report had
been implemented. Even before taking up the report, the EU had

already launched a significant investment effort through its NextGe -
nerationEU (NGEU) program: EUR 573 billion distributed via the
Recovery and Resilience Facility (RRF) between 2020 and 2026, with a
focus on energy infrastructure (particularly renewables), railway, di -
gital & connectivity 3. According to the Commission, two-thirds of the
6,000 “milestones and targets” assigned to governments have already
been linked to the RRF4.

4
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
What are the results on the ground? An analysis based solely on GDP
trends would not be conclusive: after a rebound in 2025, European
growth is projected to slow slightly in 2026. The International Mone -
tary Fund (IMF) estimates it at 1.4%, a rate nearly half that expected
in the United States.
In fact, the EU is showing remarkable resilience in the face of another

major energy shock that, in other times, might have pushed it into
recession. The German economy, one of the most exposed, is even gai-
ning momentum despite headwinds, a sign that, beneath the surface,
certain trends are gaining traction.
To assess the conflicting forces that have been shaping the Euro -
pean economy since the publication of the “Draghi” report, a sectoral
breakdown may prove useful. In particular, it allows to quantify the
ongoing transformation of the automotive industry, where a 150-year-

old technology — the internal combustion engine — is gradually giving
way to electric power.
TRANSFORMATION OF THE AUTOMOTIVE SECTOR
Year in and year out, European automakers are continuing their trans-
formation. After a lull in 2023-24 due to a decline in government subsi-
dies, European production of electric vehicles is picking up again (+30%
in 2025). In 2026, based on figures from the battery sector (production
up 25% y/y in July), this momentum continues. However, the volumes

involved (3.2 million electric vehicles assembled annually in Europe
compared to nearly 17 million in China, representing 75% of the global
market5) remain too low to offset the decline in sales in the traditional
internal combustion engine sector.
Compared to its pre-COVID average (2015-19), total European automo-
bile production has thus fallen by 30% in volume — a historic slump.
In 2026, against a backdrop of soaring fuel prices, the trend remains

negative (down 9.6% y/y in July, according to Eurostat). Upstream in the
supply chain, automotive suppliers, machine tool manufacturers, and
metal manufacturers are struggling (see Chart 1).
THE RISE OF AI
As an example of “creative destruction,” artificial intelligence is beco -
ming, alongside the energy transition, a driving force for the European
economy, as evidenced by the boom in related industries. In Germany,
the difficulties of the automotive sector stand in stark contrast to the

strong momentum in orders for electrical and electronic equipment.
In Europe, the production of IT equipment (computers, peripherals,
etc.) is growing at a rate of 40% per year — a level not seen since the
rapid digitalisation that accompanied the 2020-21 health crisis. Ge -
nerally speaking, most sectors involved in generating or transmitting
electricity (generators, transformers, cables, etc.) are currently outper-
forming the average. This is also true of the rail industry (locomotives

and other rolling stock), driven by “green” investment efforts under -
taken as part of the NGEU program.
5 Data from 2025. Source: IEA (International Energy Agency), Global EV Outlook, 2026.
6 See Derrien, G. (2026), ”When it comes to China, Europe should focus less on its trade deficits and more on its investments,” BNP Paribas EcoWeek, October 5.
DEFENCE EFFORTS
If there is one area where actions finally match commitments, it is
defence. Fighting vehicles, weapons and ammunition, as well as te -

lecommunications and optical equipment — which are ubiquitous in
drone systems — are all sectors where production volumes are brea -
king records and growth rates range from 20% to 50% annually.
***
Far from the image of stagnation it often projects, the EU has, for se -
veral years now, been making a significant effort to modernise and
strengthen the foundations of its economy. Although close to its end,
the NGEU programme will continue to support investment, while ini -

tiatives taken in the wake of the “Draghi” report are generating further
momentum. Sectoral analysis also shows that Europe is catching up
with the AI wave — admittedly belatedly, but with increasing momen-
tum.
Whether by coincidence or as a result, its hourly productivity has re -
bounded significantly over the past few quarters, returning to the 1%
annual growth rate it had before the pandemic (see Chart 2). Finally,
contrary to a fairly widespread belief, European companies have lar -

gely managed to maintain their competitiveness. Aside from the very
specific case of China, their trade in manufactured goods outside the
EU has tended to strengthen in recent years6.
Jean-Luc Proutat
jean-luc.proutat@bnpparibas.com

景気は内需主因で停滞
◆消費・投資はコロナ禍以来の低い伸び
中国景気は内需の不振で足踏み 。小売売上高
は、財消費の低迷を主因に前年並みの水準で推移。
耐久消費財の買い替え補助金の効果がはく落して
いるほか、中東危機によるガソリン価格の高騰などを
背景に消費者マインドが一段と悪化 。固定資産投
資はインフラ投資などで底打ちの兆しがみられるもの
の、昨年以降大幅な減少が継続。
一方、外需は高い伸びを維持 。世界的なAIブー
ムを背景にIT関連財の輸出が引き続き好調である
ほか、EV需要の高まりを受けて自動車輸出台数も
3カ月連続で過去最高水準。もっとも、原油や半導
体などの価格上昇の影響も大きく 、実質ベースでは、
内需の減速を補うには至らず。
◆成長率目標の達成は困難
先行きの景気も、内需を中心に停滞が続く見通し。
ハイテク製造業の投資拡大や電力 ・通信網などの
「６つのネットワーク 」推進によるインフラ投資の持ち
直しが見込まれるものの 、不動産不況や過剰生産

能力といった構造的な下押し要因が残存し 、投資
は総じて低調に推移する見込み。悲観的な消費者
マインドが続くなか、消費も低迷が続く公算大。
一方、政府は景気刺激策に慎重 。成長率よりも
経済の質を重視し 、不動産市場や地方政府債務
などのリスク解消に注力する姿勢を明確にしており 、
2026年の成長率目標は達成困難な状況。

（資料）人民網などを基に日本総研作成

消費は総じて低調
◆サービス消費は堅調も、財消費が低迷
８月の小売売上高は前年比＋ 0.3％と、ほぼゼ
ロ成長。財消費の低迷が主因であり、なかでも自動
車が大幅減。買い替え補助政策で喚起された需要
の反動減に加え、原油高でガソリン車需要が減退。
宝飾品も 、国際的な金価格の上昇と節約志向の
高まりが相まって、販売数量が大幅減。
先行きの消費も、総じて低調に推移する見通し。
消費者マインドは中東危機を契機に悪化基調 。６
月に一時持ち直したものの 、ガソリン価格の再上昇
を受けて再び低下 。政府は利子補給制度の対象
拡大などで内需を喚起しているものの 、大規模な消
費刺激策は打ち出さない姿勢。一方、サービス消費
は今後の消費拡大の重点分野に位置づけられてお
り、堅調に推移する見込み。
◆住宅市場は長期停滞から脱却できず
住宅市場は引き続き低迷。大都市を中心に住宅
購入要件の緩和などが実施されているものの 、購入
層の減少といった構造的な要因が住宅販売の重石

に。新築住宅価格に加え 、市場の実勢を反映しや
すい中古住宅価格も引き続き下落。在庫の回転期
間も長期化している模様。住宅市場の健全化に向
け、予約販売から完成後販売への移行や住宅ロー
ン返済期間の延長などが打ち出されたものの 、効果
が短期間で現れるとは見込めず 、住宅市場の本格
回復にはなお数年を要する見通し。

◆投資は下げ止まりの兆し
８月の固定資産投資は前年比 ▲10.6％と、大
幅減が続いているものの、一部で底打ちの兆し。セク
ター別にみると 、インフラ投資は 、「６つのネットワー
ク」の推進などを背景にマイナス幅が縮小。一方、製
造業投資は減少幅が拡大 。政府は経済運営の質
を重視する姿勢をとっており、過剰生産能力の解消
が投資の重石になっている可能性。不動産投資も、
住宅市場の低迷を背景に大幅減少が継続。
◆年末にかけてマイナス幅が縮小
先行きの投資は、以下２点から減少幅が縮小す
る見通し。第１に、ハイテク製造業の投資拡大。政
府による支援も相まって、半導体やロボットなど先端
分野は増産基調。第２に、インフラ投資の持ち直し。
8,000億元の新型政策性金融ツールと「６つのネッ
トワーク（六張網）」が年後半のインフラ投資を押し
上げ。六張網は官民が重点的にインフラ整備を進め
る戦略分野であり 、既存整備計画の前倒しのみな
らず、次世代通信網や計算能力網などの新規計画

も立ち上げ。足元では、情報伝送や鉄道輸送 、航
空輸送に関連する固定資産投資に底打ちの兆し。
もっとも、本格回復は見込めない状況 。製造業や
不動産業では、過剰な生産能力や在庫などの構造
的な下押し要因が残存 。インフラ投資も量的拡大
は限定的。財源となる地方債は借り換えや隠れ債
務の圧縮が優先され、新発債の発行は停滞。

外需は先行きも堅調
◆輸出入ともに価格上昇の影響大
８月の名目輸出は前年比＋ 24.9％と、高い伸
び。ASEAN・NIEs向けでは、IT関連財の輸出が引
き続き好調であるほか 、中東危機による原油供給
懸念を背景にEV輸出も拡大。加えて、対米輸出が
米中関係の改善を受けて堅調に推移しているほか、
EU向け輸出も、年初ほどの勢いはないものの底堅く
推移。こうした輸出額の増加には 、価格上昇の影
響も大。原油価格が高止まりしているほか 、AIブー
ムを背景に半導体や銅 、金属鉱石などの価格も上
昇。実質ベースの輸出の伸びは弱く、内需の減速を
補うには至らず。
先行きも、外需は堅調に推移する見通し。９月の
米中首脳会談でも貿易摩擦の緩和に向けた認識
で一致しており、対米輸出は前年比プラスで推移す
る見通し。また、中東危機を受けて世界的な脱炭
素化が一段と加速しており 、EV輸出が拡大する公
算大。ASEANなどへの中間財輸出も 、AIブームを

背景に好調を維持する見込み。
◆資金流出は拡大傾向
金融収支は流出傾向が継続 。内需の不振で対
内直接投資が減少しているほか 、準備資産も縮小。
商業銀行などへの外貨資金の振替・預託や資産再
配分が進行している可能性 。先行きも、内需の低
迷が対内投資の重石となり 、金融収支でみた資本
流出傾向が継続する見通し。

のの、中東情勢の不透明感や内需の弱さが相場の
重石に。景気が停滞局面に入る なか、先行きも弱
い動きが続く見込み。

## Document 536: 全球央行囤金潮持续！高盛强调：重要的不只是“买”，还有“转”

Source PDF: all report pdf/全球央行囤金潮持续！高盛强调：重要的不只是“买”，还有“转”.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

全球央⾏购⾦速度依然是 2022 年前历史均值的近五倍，⾼盛据此将⻩⾦⽬标价设为 5400 美
元 / 盎司。该⾏指出，当前更值得关注的变化是⻩⾦的储存地点 —— ⼤量⻩⾦正从纽约联储
流向英格兰银⾏，荷兰等国明确以 “ 危机准备 ” 为由完成转移。这⼀趋势说明，央⾏对托管⽅
的信任格局正在悄然重构。
摘要：
据⾼盛分析师 Lina Thomas 发布的最新⽉度预测报告，全球央⾏ 8 ⽉购⾦量估计达 79 吨，远⾼于
2022 年前 17 吨的历史⽉均⽔平，也⾼于 7 ⽉的 44 吨。以三个⽉季节性调整后的⼝径计算，当前
购⾦速度为每⽉ 85 吨，折合年化约 1000 吨 —— ⽽ 2022 年俄罗斯外汇储备遭冻结之前，这⼀数字
仅约 200 吨。
中国央⾏持续增持⻩⾦储备，依然是 8 ⽉最⼤的可识别买家。⾼盛在报告中写道： “ 新兴市场央
⾏，尤其是中国，相对于其合理的⻓期配置⽬标，⻩⾦持仓仍明显偏低。 ”
更值得关注的信号：⻩⾦存在哪⾥
Lina Thomas 指出，发达国家央⾏⼤多在 2022 年后置⾝于购⾦潮之外，部分原因是它们本已持

有⼤量历史储备。但当前的讨论正在发⽣转变 ——" 从是否持有⻩⾦，转向⻩⾦存放在哪⾥ " ，这
背后指向的是对储备安全和跨国信任的更深层担忧。
数据印证了这⼀判断。 8 ⽉，存放于纽约联储的外国央⾏⻩⾦减少 40 吨，同期英格兰银⾏托管量
增加 46 吨。 7 ⽉，英格兰银⾏托管量已跳升 63 吨。 9 ⽉初，荷兰央⾏披露，已以 " 危机准备 " 为
由，将近 90 吨⻩⾦从纽约和渥太华转移⾄伦敦。
⾃ 2025 年初以来，纽约联储托管量累计下降约 200 吨，英格兰银⾏托管量则累计上升约 500 吨。
⾼盛还提⽰了⼀个对多头有利的技术细节：其基于伦敦的预测模型并不涵盖英格兰银⾏托管量
的变化。当英格兰银⾏托管增量超过纽约联储托管降量时，实际官⽅购⾦规模可能⽐预测数字
更⼤。 8 ⽉，英格兰银⾏增加 46 吨，纽约联储减少 40 吨，差值虽然收窄，但⽅向⼀致。
⾦价尚未跟上，⾼盛仍守 5400 美元⽬标
上述购⾦潮发⽣之际，⾦价⾛势却相对沉寂。据⾼盛材料部⻔数据，现货⾦价当⽇约为 4186 美

元 / 盎司，低于⾼盛上⽉下调年底公允价值时约 4350 美元的⽔平，也逐渐逼近该⾏在美联储鹰派
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

的看法，不如说是在表达对托管⽅的信任判断。

缘政治、对 G7 储备货币的不信任 —— 并未消退。 ”
